[For approximately twenty (20) minutes before the board meeting, all 
the monitors in the room will display a visual recognition of the 
District’s retirees.  A note card wi ll be placed on a chair for each retiree 
attending the board meeting.  Jasen Witt  will read the name of each 
retiree, his/her position and length of career in the District.  The retiree 
will come forward and stand near the presentation table when his/her 
name is read.  Board President Wendy Underwood  will stand at the 
presentation table and present each retiree with a retirement gift (clock) 
and a certificate of appreciation.]

 Whereas, Pam Byrd started as a Teacher’s Assistant for

service to the students and staff of the Troy School

Comments Mrs. Fleming invited everyone to visit Somerset North to 
see the 34 th Annual Student Art Show.  The show runs 
from May 3 -16, 2010, and there is some amazing  student 
artwork on display. 
Mrs. Fleming also informed everyone about the upcoming 
Town Hall Meeting for concerned parents and citizens 
entitled, “The Sexting Society” at the Perspectives 
Counseling Center, 888 W. Big Beaver, Troy, Michigan, on 
May 13, 2010, from 7:00 -9:00 p.m.  Troy Community 
Coalition, Troy Police Departme nt, Troy School District, 
Troy Youth Assistance, and Perspectives are sponsoring 
the event.  If interested in attending, p lease call 248.244 -
8644 to reserve a seat. 
Dr. Fowler mentioned the following successes that the 
District celebrated this past week:   
 Two students at Athens High School received a perfect 
ACT score, one of wh o also earned a perfect SAT 
mathematics score, and a Troy High student received a 
perfect ACT score. 
 Mrs. Davisson and Dr. Fowler attended a recognition 
ceremony at Oakland Schools  where 13 of our schools 
were awarded “ Green Schools ” honors.  This is in 
addition to two of our schools that earned the same 
recognition last year.   
 Nearly 5,000 s tudents in grades 4 through 12 were 
recognized at our annual “Salute to Excellence ” at 
Somerset North.  As Mrs. Fleming mentioned, there is 
currently an art exhibit at Somerset of more than 400

BOARD WORKSHOP     MINUTES  MAY 11, 2010 
    ADMINISTRATIVE CENTER  
President Underwood called the board workshop to order at 6:00 p.m.  In addition to Ms. 
Underwood, the following Board members were present:  Mrs. Edmunds, Mrs. Fleming, Mr. Hauff, 
Mr. Miletti, Mrs. Philippart and Mrs. Pochodylo.  Also in attendance were Superintendent Fowler, 
administrators Adamczyk, Davisson, Hannon, Keeling, Lams, Machesky, McAvoy, Mull, Papasian, and 
Witt.  Mrs. Gayle Moran and Mr. Mike Miller from Sodexo were also present, as well as two 
members of the community. 
The following items were discussed: 
1. Public Communication* 
2. Food Service Update 
3. Assisted Technology Report - Special Education  
4. District MEAP Results Review 
5. Schools of Choice and Enrollment Update 
6. Preliminary 2010/2011 Budget 
7. Other 
8. Adjournment 
The meeting adjourned at 11:40 p.m. 
 Respectfully submitted, 
 Nancy L. Philippart, Secretary

 7. A.   05.18.10 
7. CURRICULUM 
A. REPORT:  District MEAP Results  
The Troy School District has a comprehensive 
assessment system that includes district and school 
developed common assessments as well as other 
student achievement measures.  One part of our 
district’s assessment program is the Michigan 
Educational Assessment Program —also called “the 
MEAP.” 
The MEAP assesses students in grades 3  through 9 
in math, reading, science, and social studies.  
Results of the MEAP are used to monitor 
continuous improvement.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report will be given 
by Anne Mull, Director of Instruction and Assessment .

                  7. B.    05.18.10 
7. CURRICULUM 
B. CONSIDERATION:   High School Graduation Requirements and  Grade 
Point Average Calculations 
Changes to the Michigan Merit Curriculum requirements and the district’s goal of 
providing more flexibility and choice in student scheduling has resulted in a 
recommended change when credit is granted for World Language courses.  The 
district also recognizes the need for all high school juniors to participate in the 
Michigan Merit Exam (MME) given in the spring of each school year.  
Additionally, it is recommended that courses identified as Advanced Placement 
(AP) be given extra weighting when calculating a student grade point average 
(GPA).  It is recommended that a change in our Troy School District graduation 
requirements and grade point average calculations be made to reflect this 
change in policy.  Therefore, it would be appropriate at this time for the Board to 
adopt the following resolution. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
  Moved By:   
  Supported By:  
be it therefore resolved that all juniors must participate in the Michigan Merit Exam in order 
to fulfil requirements toward graduation. Effective with the class of 2014, students taking and 
passing full year world language courses prior to high school will receive credit toward meeting 
the Michigan Merit Curriculum and Troy School District graduation requirements. Effective with 
the class of 2011 and subsequent graduating classes, courses identified as Advanced Placement 
(AP) will be given extra weighting when calculating a student’s grade point average (GPA).  
Adjusted grade point averages for all impacted students will be reflected on student transcripts 
beginning August 2010. 
Ayes:  
Nays:

MEMORANDUM 
To: Frank Lams, Purchasing Supervisor  
From: Scott Bryan, Director of Technology  
Date: May 5, 2010 
Re: Phone system upgrades 
Attach: Suntel Letter 
The software running on the District’s existing phone systems has been announced as end -of-life by Mitel, 
the equipment manufacturer, and our exist ing hardware does not meet the technical requirements to run 
supported versions of software.  Therefore, we released bid #9669 on April 7, with bids opened on April 21, 
2010.  We received one bid response, one no bid, and one notice of bid was returned und eliverable.  Like 
many manufacturers, Mitel has a limited partner channel that permits only certain integrators to provide bid 
responses.  The lone response from SunTel Services is from the only Mitel integrator in the area.   
SunTel’s bid response as read at the bid opening was $51,600.  Upon evaluating the bid, I noticed some 
obvious mathematical errors:  the per -unit trade in rebate was added instead of subtracted to the base bid, and 
the base bid also included alternates. I met with SunTel to review th ese issues, and received a written letter 
of clarification and corrected pricing sheet (see attached).  I have verified that none of the individual prices 
have been changed, but now all of the prices fall into the correct categories to match our bid form.  
The correct base bid response from SunTel is $49,708.41.  I am also recommending acceptance of all 
alternates as follows (MA – mandatory alternate requested in bid, VA – voluntary proposed by SunTel):  
MA-1, extend warranty to three years for all replaced switches: $7,096.50 
MA-2, accept trade-in deduction: -$7,125 
MA-3, Career Center upgrade (approved by Dorothy Kaltz): $1,485.90  
VA-4, extend software maintenance to 2 nd year: $5,348 
VA-5, extend warranty on existing switches (High Schools & Baker Middle): $2,241 
VA-6, accept SunTel additional discount: -$5,500 
Subtotal alternates: $3,546.40 
Starting with the base bid of $49,708.4 1 and adding all of the alternates as described results in a total project 
price of $53,254.81.  Mike Adamczyk has approved the acceptance of all alternates.  
This item requires Board action due to the dollar amount of the purchase; please place this on the  May 18, 
2010 agenda.  Thank you for your attention to this matter.  
100

Door Replacement Project. A copy of the bid tabulation is enclosed for your review.  The 
apparent low bid was received from Peterson Glass Co. of Ferndale, Michigan for a total 
combined bid of $806,000.  The following is our itemized recommendation for your 
consideration:

Recommendation:   Reject Bid

 Recommendation:        Reject Bid

 Recommendation:        Reject Bid

G. CONSIDERATION: Resolution to Hold a Public 
Hearing on the Proposed 2010-
2011 School Year Budget 
________________________________________________________ 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: _______________________________ 
Supported By:  ___________________________ 
WHEREAS, Michigan law provides that every local unit of 
government, including a school district, shall hold a public hearing on 
its proposed budget, with notice of such at least six (6) days before the 
hearing; and 
WHEREAS, such notice is required to include the time and place of 
the hearing and also state the place where a copy of the proposed 
budget is available for public inspection; and 
WHEREAS, the statutory provision als o requires that the notice 
include in 11-point bold faced type, the following statement:   
THE PROPERTY TAX MILLAGE RATE PROPOSED TO BE 
LEVIED TO SUPPORT THE PROPOSED BUDGET WILL BE 
A SUBJECT OF THIS HEARING. 
171
8. G.    5.18.10 
WHEREAS, each local unit is required to hold  the public hearing 
prior to final adoption of its budget. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
1. A public hearing shall be held on June 15, 20 10 at a regular 
Board meeting beginning at 7: 00 p.m. at the Troy School 
District’s Services Building, 4420 Livernois Road, Troy 48098, 
for the purpose of receiving testimony and discussing the 
proposed 2010-2011 budget. 
2. The Secretary of the Board of Education is hereby authorized 
and directed to publish notice of such public hearing, said 
notice to be in substantially the form of attached Exhibit A, in a 
newspaper of general circulation within the School District.  
The notice shall be published at least six (6) days before the 
public hearing and shall comply with all requirements of  
the applicable statutory provisions. 
Ayes:  _________________ 
Nays:  _________________ 
Resolution declared adopted.

     10. A.    05.18.10 
10. OTHER 
 A. CONSIDERATION:  Approve Oakland Intermediate 
School District Budget 
  In accordance with Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as 
amended, the Troy Board of Education is required to review 
the proposed Oakland Intermediate School District budget and 
express either its support or disapproval of this budget.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution.  
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
WHEREAS: 
 1. Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended , requires the 
intermediate school board to submit its proposed budget no later than May 1 
of each year to the board of each constituent district for review; and  
 2. Not later than June 1 of each year, the board of each constituent district shall 
review the proposed intermediate school district budget, shall adopt a board 
resolution expressing its support for or disapproval of the proposed 
intermediate school district budget, and shall submit to the intermediate 
school board any specific objections and pro posed changes the constituent 
district board has to the budget. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT:  
 1. The Troy Board of Education has received and reviewed the proposed 
intermediate school district budget in accordance with Section 624 of the 
Revised School Code, as amended, and, by the adoption of this resolution, 
174
expresses its support for the proposed intermediate school district budget.  
The Troy School District Board of Education believes that it is the responsibility 
of the Intermediate School Dis trict to be responsible for its own operations, 
including adoption of its budget.  The Troy School District’s support of the 
Oakland Intermediate School District’s budget is for the sole purpose of 
complying with Section 624 of the Revised School Code.  
 2. The secretary of the Troy Board of Education or his/her designee shall forward 
a copy of this resolution to the intermediate school board or its superintendent 
no later than June 1, 2010. 
 3. All resolutions insofar as they conflict with this resolution  be and the same are 
hereby rescinded. 
Ayes:  
Nays:  
Resolution declared adopted. 
 Secretary, Troy Board of Education  
The undersigned duly qualified and acting Secretary of the Board of Education of the 
Troy School District, Michigan, hereby  certifies that the foregoing is a true and 
complete copy of a resolution adopted by the Troy Board of Education at a regular 
meeting held on May 18, 2010, the original of which resolution is part of the Board’s 
minutes, and further certifies that notice o f the meeting was given to the public 
under the Open Meetings Act, 1976 PA 267, as amended.  
 Secretary, Troy Board of Education

         10. B.      05.18.10  
10. OTHER  
 B. Race to the Top Memorandum of Understanding  
A RESOLUTION  of the Board of Education of the Troy School District  to 
encourage and empower the Superintendent and the President of the School 
Board to sign Michigan’s Race to the Top Memorandum of Understanding . 
WHEREAS, through the passage of legislation in 2009 and 2010 Michigan 
has made great strides in education reform, and  
WHEREAS, the Board of Education and the Superintendent of the Troy 
School District believe these reforms are necessary to improve the 
academic achievement of our students; and  
WHEREAS, the Race to the Top application is based on four focus areas 
that are critical to increasing the achievement and success of our 
students; and  
WHEREAS , those four  areas are Standards & Assessments; Data Systems 
to Support Instruction ; Great Teachers & Leaders; and Turning Around the 
Low Performing Schools; and  
WHEREAS, the Troy School District has work underway in each of these areas; 
and 
WHEREAS, our work could be implemented faster with the addition of federal 
funds focused in these areas; and  
WHEREAS , a successful Race to the Top application is estimated to bring 
$400 million  dollars to the state of Michigan to complete the required 
components of the Memorandum o f Understanding ; and 
WHEREAS, the required components of the Memorandum of 
Understanding include outcomes designed to modify and enhance the 
usefulness of our data systems to teachers; comply with state law providing 
measures of student growth to educators; and intervening in struggling 
schools per the federally approved intervention models; and  
WHEREAS, a successful Race to the Top application will include 
approval from Superintendents, School Boards, and Union P artners; 
and 
179
WHEREAS, the method for communicating that approval is to sign the state’s 
Memorandum of Understanding. 
NOW THEREFORE, BE IT  RESOLVED, that by this Resolution the Board of 
Education authorizes and directs the President of the School Board and School 
Superintendent to sign the Memorandum of Understanding; and therefore, be it 
further  
RESOLVED, that the Superintendent and President of the School Board shall sign 
the Memorandum of Understanding no later than May ____ 2010, so that the 
Memorandum of Understanding can be submitted to the MD E by the 
Intermediate School District prior to the May 21, 2010 , deadline; and 
therefore, be it further  
RESOLVED,  that copies of this resolution shall be presented to  the President of 
the Troy Education Association and request that the agreement be execut ed by 
the labor organization(s) .

10. OTHER 
 C. CONSIDERATION:   Resolution in Support of the Oakland   
    Community College Millage Renewal 
In August of 2010, Oakland Community College will ask the residents of 
Oakland County to renew their property tax commitment of eight-tenths of 
one mill for ten years to support college operations, rolled back by the 
Headlee Amendment.  The millage money will enable OCC to maintain its 
pivotal role in Oakland County of providing students and incumbent 
employees with quality, affordable, state-of-the-art education and training 
that will equip them to take and maintain their places in the dynamic 
workforce of the 21st century.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:   Adopt the following resolution. 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
 WHEREAS, Oakland Community College has made a lasting impact on our 
community by educating more than 700,000 students of all groups since its 
establishment in 1964, and 
181
 WHEREAS, Oakland Community College continues to serve its community 
through a comprehensive array of offerings including career programs, university 
transfer programs, and workforce development services, and  
 WHEREAS, the renewal of the ten-year .8 mill property tax levy in the August 3, 
2010 primary is critical to the college’s ability to maintain the level of services required by 
the people of Oakland County. 
 NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that the Board of the Troy School 
District urges support for the renewal of Oakland Community College’s .8 tax levy so that 
it may continue operations that are so critical to the future economic recovery and 
success of our region.

Oakland Schools’ Board of Education Biennial Election Information  
Three (3) individuals have filed as candidates for the three seats open on the Oakland Schools ’ 
Board of Education Biennial Election.  All three individuals are current trustees (incumbents) of the 
Oakland Schools Board.  The three candidates and the terms they are seeking are:  
Ms. Barbara DeMarco (6-year term) 
Mr. Connie Williams (6-year term) 
Dr. Theresa Rich (partial 2-year term) 
Oakland Schools board members are elected by an electoral body composed of 1 (one) 
person designated by the board of each constituent school district. The Revised School Code 
prescribes that each constituent school district board must designate a representati ve to this 
electoral body by adopting a resolution that will 1) designate the person to vote in the 
election on behalf of the local district board, 2) direct the designee which candidate the 
board supports for each position to be filled, 3) direct the desi gnee to vote for those 
individuals, at least on the first ballot taken by the electoral body.  The designated electors 
must cast their ballots  in person at the election scheduled to take place between 6:30 – 7:15 
PM on Monday, June 7 in Conference Room A o f the Oakland Schools building located at 
2111 Pontiac Lake Road in Waterford.   The revised school code prohibits the 
acceptance of absentee ballots.  
According to the revised school code, districts shall consider the resolution at not less than 
one (1) me eting before adopting the resolution. Districts have interpreted this language 
differently and historically have used one of the following three options to consider and 
adopt their resolutions: 
 Consider the resolution as a discussion item during an open m eeting and adopt the 
resolution as an action item during another open meeting, with each meeting 
occurring on a separate date 
 Consider the resolution as a discussion item during an open meeting and adopt the 
resolution as an action item during a second ope n meeting immediately following the 
first open meeting 
 Consider the resolution as a discussion item and adopt the resolution as an action 
item at the same open meeting (Local school districts selecting this option should 
seek independent legal counsel conf irming a singular open meeting to consider and 
adopt a resolution is compliant with the revised school code).  
According to the revised school code, districts must adopt their resolutions no earlier than 
21 days prior to the election (no earlier than May 17, 2010).  
After your board has adopted its resolution as prescribed by the revised school code, 
please fax (248.209.2206) a copy of the resolution to the Oakland Schools’ 
Superintendent’s office by the end of the business day on Friday, June 4, 2010 . This 
will enable us to compile a list of district designees prior to the election date.  
184
Attached is a copy of the Revised School Code, a sample resolution template to designate 
your district’s representative and selection of candidates, candidate profiles, a nd an election 
timeline. 
An official notice of this election will be sent to your board of education secretary via 
registered mail next week. 
If you have any questions regarding the ISD legislation regarding board elections or the ISD 
board election process please contact Sherry McMillan at 248.209.2062 or by email at 
Sherry.McMillan@oakland.k12.mi.us .  
Note: as the candidates are running un -opposed and are incumbents, the “Meet the 
Candidates” forum scheduled for this evening has been cancelled.

Oakland Schools Board of Education 2010 Biennial Election 
Candidate Profiles 
Ms. Barbara DeMarco 
Ms. Barbara A. DeMarco of West Bloomfield currently holds the office of Secretary on 
the Oakland Schools Board of Education and concurrently serves as the Trustee of the 
West Bloomfield Board of Education. She is a member of the Oakland Schools Legislative 
Committee, Oakland County School Boards Association, and serves on the Education 
Advisory Group (EAG) for Career Focused Education. Barb is self-employed designing, 
developing and maintaining information technology systems.  
Appointed to the Oakland Schools Board of Education on March 16, 2004, elected to a 6-year term on 
June 7, 2004.  Seeking a 6-year term 
Mr. Connie R. Williams, CPA, FHFMA 
Mr. Connie R. Williams currently holds the office of Vice President on the Oakland 
Schools Board of Education and also served on the Southfield Public Schools Board of 
Education from 2004 - 2009, serving as its President and Vice President. Connie is a 
Certified Public Accountant (CPA) and a Fellow in the Health Care Financial 
Management Association (FHFMA). He has served as an elected member of National 
Board of the HFMA and as a member of its Principles and Practices Board. He is the 
Corporate Compliance and Privacy Officer of the Botsford Health Care Corporation of 
Farmington Hills.  Connie is a graduate of Wayne State University’s School of Business 
Administration and is a member of Beta Alpha Psi, The National Accounting Fraternity. 
Connie and his wife Margie have two daughters: one attending the University of 
Michigan and the other attending the University of Southern California. 
Appointed to the Oakland Schools Board of Education on October 2, 2007, elected to a two-year term on 
June 7, 2008. Seeking a 6-year term 
186
Dr. Theresa Rich 
Dr. Theresa Rich, of Farmington Hills, was appointed as trustee by the Oakland Schools 
Board of Education on February 15, 2010. She is a veteran employee of General Motors 
who works in Human Resources/Organizational Development. Dr. Rich has extensive 
board and commission experience, including service on the Farmington Hills 
Commission on Children, Youth & Families, Farmington Pledge Committee and 
Farmington Hills/Farmington Foundation for Youth & Families. 
Dr. Rich received her MBA/BBA in Accounting from Grand Valley State University, LL.M 
(Taxation) and JD from Wayne State University Law School, and Ph.D. (Organization 
and Management) from Capella University. She is married to Brian Rich and has two 
children, Zachary and Alyson.  
Appointed February 15, 2010.  Seeking partial 2-year term

Oakland Schools Board of Education  
Election 2010 Timeline 
March 1 – May 7:   
Candidates may pick up nominating petitions and affidavit forms at the Oakland County 
Clerk’s office, Elections Division, 1200 N. Telegraph Road, Dept. 417, Pontiac, 48341. 
Monday, May 10, 4 PM:  
Last day for candidates to file nominating petitions, filing fees, and affidavits of identity 
with the Oakland County Clerk’s office. 
Tuesday, May 11: 
Oakland Schools receives list of qualified candidates from Oakland County Clerk’s 
office. 
Wednesday, May 12:   
Oakland Schools sends list of qualified candidates with background information to 
constituent district superintendents and board of education secretaries. 
Thursday, May 13: 
“Meet the Candidates” Forum, 7 PM at the Oakland Schools administration building, 
2111 Pontiac Lake Road, Waterford, Michigan (sponsored by OCSBA). 
May 17 – June 3:  
Each constituent district board of education must 1) designate its representative and 
identify the ISD candidates its board supports, and 2) consider and adopt a resolution 
affirming #1. Each board must consider its resolution at not less than one (1) public 
meeting before adopting the resolution.   
Friday, June 4:  
Copies of district resolutions due to the Oakland Schools administrative office via fax 
248.209.2206. 
Monday, June 7, 6:30 – 7:15 PM: 
District representatives attend Oakland Schools Board of Education open meeting to cast 
ballots on behalf of their districts.  No absentee ballots can be accepted. 
Tuesday, June 8: 
Notification of election results sent from Oakland Schools to district superintendents and 
Oakland County Clerk. 
Tuesday, July 6: 
Successful candidates take oath of office at Oakland Schools Board of Education 
Organizational Meeting.

in June by an electoral body composed of 1 person designated by the board of each constituent school district.
(2) The board of a constituent district shall designate its representative to this electoral body by resolution
adopted not earlier than 21 days before the date of this biennial election. The board shall consider the
resolution at not less than 1 public meeting before adopting the resolution. The resolution shall be adopted by
majority vote of the members serving on the board. In its resolution designating its representative, the board
of a constituent district shall identify the candidate the board supports for each position to be filled on the
intermediate school board and shall direct its representative to vote for that individual or individuals at least
on the first ballot taken by the electoral body. The secretary of the intermediate school board shall send a
notice by certified mail of the hour and place of the meeting of the electoral body described in subsection (1)
to the secretary of the board of each constituent school district at least 10 days before the meeting. The
president and secretary of the intermediate school board shall act as chairperson and secretary at the meeting.
The meeting of the electoral body shall be an open meeting conducted in the manner prescribed under the
open meetings act, 1976 PA 267, MCL 15.261 to 15.275.
(3) Except as provided in section 703, the term of office of each member elected to the intermediate school
board is 6 years and begins on July 1 following election. Not more than 2 members of the intermediate school
board shall be from the same school district unless there are fewer districts than there are positions to be
filled.
(4) A vacancy shall be filled by the remaining members of the intermediate school board until the next
biennial election at which time the vacancy shall be filled for the balance of the unexpired term. Notice of the
vacancy shall be filed with the state board within 5 days after the vacancy occurs. If the vacancy is not filled
within 30 days after it occurs, the vacancy shall be filled by the state board.
(5) Subject to subsection (7), a candidate for election to the intermediate school board shall be nominated
by petitions that are signed by a number of school electors of the combined constituent school districts of the
intermediate school district, as follows:
(a) If the population of the intermediate school district is less than 10,000 according to the most recent
federal census, a minimum of 6 and a maximum of 20.
(b) If the population of the intermediate school district is 10,000 or more according to the most recent
federal census, a minimum of 40 and a maximum of 100.
(6) A school elector may sign as many petitions as there are vacancies to fill. Nominating petitions and an
affidavit as provided in section 558 of the Michigan election law, 1954 PA 116, MCL 168.558, shall be filed
with the school district filing official not later than 30 days before the date of the biennial election under
subsection (1). The school district filing official shall determine the sufficiency of the petitions and the
eligibility of the candidates nominated. The school district filing official shall provide ballots for the biennial
election, listing on the ballots the names of all candidates properly nominated. The chairperson of the biennial
election meeting may accept nominations for a vacancy from the floor only if no nominating petitions have
been filed for the vacancy.
(7) Instead of filing nominating petitions, a candidate for election to the intermediate school board may pay
a nonrefundable filing fee of $100.00 to the school district filing official. If this fee is paid by the due date for
nominating petitions, the payment has the same effect under this section as the filing of nominating petitions.
